BERITA BAHASA INDONESIA
TOK PISIN
analysis
By Matt Brann
Landline
Topic:Beef Cattle Farming
Cattle prices have soared this week thanks to rain. (ABC Landline)
Cattle prices jumped this week as heavy rain fell across key production areas in Queensland and northern New South Wales.
Meat and Livestock Australia's (MLA) benchmark Eastern Young Cattle Indicator (EYCI) surged beyond 1,000 cents a kilogram, as restockers and backgrounders competed for numbers.
""The cattle market shifted to a new gear off the back of this rain,"" Stuart Bull, MLA's market information manager said.
Demand for younger cattle was strong this week and the processor cow indicator hit a record high. (ABC Landline)
At Roma on Tuesday, some weaner steers from the Winton region sold for an incredible $7 a kilogram, which equated to more than $1,900 a head.
At Dubbo, the local saleyard report said restocker demand ""sparked a market boom"".
""Restocker demand was a stand-out, with young cattle selling up to an impressive 80 cents per kilo dearer [than last week],"" the report said.
""Young steers reached a top of $7.40/kg, while young restocker heifers sold to $6.50/kg, showcasing the strength of competition and confidence among buyers looking to replenish their herds.""
Scott Loughnan, who runs a mixed farming operation west of Roma, had received more than 160 millimetres of rain when he spoke to ABC News.
""It was apparently never going to rain again, but this will definitely make it easier.""
Mr Loughnan was in the middle of harvesting wheat, barley and chickpeas, and said for winter crops in the area, the rain was ""not very good"".
""I looked at some chickpeas this morning and they might not be much good, but I'm trying to look at the positives that this rain will grow us some grass and give us moisture for the next planting.""
The MLA national indicators for processor cows and heavy steers have both reached record highs this week. (ABC Landline)
Meanwhile, MLA's national processor cow indicator has cracked $4 a kilogram for the first time on record, and the national heavy steer indicator has also reached a new high of $4.77 a kilogram.
Global AgriTrends livestock analyst Simon Quilty said the supply of heavy steers was tight and processors were competing against feedlots to secure numbers.
""Once upon a time, feedlot operators wanted 450 kilogram to 500 kilogram steers, that was the preferred weight,"" he said.
""But that's now been lifted because of the lack of supply and they're taking 550 kilograms to almost 630 kilograms, creating an overlap [in demand] between processors and their needs for slaughter-ready cattle and the feedlotters who have extended the requirements of their cattle.
Mr Quilty believes Australia's cattle industry is at the start of a herd rebuild and is forecasting cattle prices to increase next year.
He is forecasting heavy steer prices to rise by about 20 per cent to about $5.70/kg next year.
Watch ABC TV's Landline at 12:30pm on Sunday or on ABC iview.

More from the Review

																	October 24, 2026
															
James Glossop/Wellcome Collection
Gavin Francis
James Glossop/Wellcome Collection
Gavin Francis

This article is part of a regular series of conversations with the Review’s contributors; read past entries here and sign up for our email newsletter to get them delivered to your inbox each week.
Among the bodybuilders Gavin Francis discusses in his essay from the October 22 issue of the Review, the most surprising is surely Leopold Bloom, the hero of Ulysses, who keeps in a drawer (along with his stash of pornographic postcards) a chart measuring his progress with “Sandow-Whiteley’s pulley exerciser,” a real device invented by the man who organized the first strongman competitions in Great Britain. (Bloom’s “biceps 9 in and 10 in” probably did not qualify.) The dream of getting shredded is not new, but the idea of breaking the body to build it back stronger has perhaps never been taken so literally as it is by today’s bodybuilders. From unregulated steroids that reduce lifespan to “looksmaxxing” exercises that require smashing one’s own bones, bodybuilding is suffering from “an epidemic of abuse,” Francis writes, “evolving into a public health emergency, quickened by digital culture.”
Francis is a physician in Scotland who has seen the fallout from this abuse firsthand. Since 2014 he has written for the Review on public health subjects ranging from vaccine skepticism to mental health diagnoses and prostate cancer. His writing about medicine, Oliver Sacks, the NHS, and much else has also appeared in The Guardian, Lancet, and the London Review of Books, among other publications, and he is the author of eleven books, including, most recently, The Unfragile Mind: A Physician’s Call for Restoring Hope and Humanity to Mental Health Care, which published earlier this year. The first episode of Gigantic Fantastic, his radio series about the bodybuilding world, will be broadcast on BBC Radio 4 on November 16.
I wrote to Francis this week to discuss steroid abuse, shrinking testes, and the public health crisis fueled by gray-market drugs. 
Chandler Fritz: In your work as a physician you’ve seen many young men who are using unregulated steroids. When did the uptick in these patients begin? 
Gavin Francis: The increase among young men who are not athletes or serious bodybuilders began to accelerate with the rise of social media over the last ten years—before that the men who used anabolic steroids were more likely to be competing at sports or involved in bodybuilding itself. Staff who work at harm reduction clinics set up specifically to help men who use steroids tell me that twenty years ago there were fewer users, and those users took longer breaks between cycles at lower doses. There’s a culture now of not only increasing doses but maintaining high doses for longer periods of time in the background, so that some men never take a break from added steroids. We’re also seeing perfectly fit young men wanting extra testosterone, styled as “testosterone replacement therapy,” even though their testosterone levels are physiologically normal. 
Young men have always been vulnerable to content that suggests that they could get better results in the gym if only they would try some commercial product or other, and today those products include testosterone, synthetic steroids, substances that mimic testosterone, human growth hormone, or experimental peptides. The market for profiting off male body anxiety is nothing new. But the amazing reach of the Internet, which offers near-limitless sources of often conflicting information, combined with the speed of biotechnology developments, has created a world in which it’s harder to convince people of an evidence-based position. Instead, people tend to trust the influencers (or, as I heard them called recently, “illfluencers”) who don’t challenge but reinforce a particular view. And so in this field, as in so many other fields of cultural life now, we are seeing greater polarization and entrenched positions. Someone can always point to a fringe view that’s backed up by a different source somewhere on the Internet.
Part of what I find fascinating about steroid use is that it is a kind of paradoxical habit: to become a “manlier man,” a guy takes drugs that, at least in reproductive terms, shrink his manhood. What does the willingness to take on the risk of infertility tell you about the desire to use steroids? 
Our bodies are amazing, and the balance of different forces of growth and repair is held in part by our hormones. When your body senses that you have too much circulating steroid, it gives up on the production of your natural testosterone in an attempt to bring the levels back to a normal physiological state, and that’s why taking external, “exogenous” steroids causes a man’s testicles to shrink.
Often the men don’t care initially about their fertility because paternity and fatherhood and family life are not something that male gym culture takes much notice of—it’s largely focused on muscle appearances and work with weights—and it seems a very distant concern for young people. Men see other men online who they admire, and their priority is how quickly they can achieve a particular look, and their substance abuse is exacerbated as they come to rely on more drugs to recover from muscle injury after strenuous workouts. It’s only later, if and when they want to start a family, that the issue arises, and often the medical services they come into contact with—endocrinologists and fertility doctors—hear these men express regret for their past usage pattern. That regret is less visible online because men don’t tend to shout about failing fertility. It’s considered unmanly to mention it, but when you work in the medical profession you know how common it is.
Advertisement
Many people are familiar with the performance-enhancing drugs used by athletes like Ben Johnson, Barry Bonds, and Lance Armstrong on their way to breaking records. What’s the difference between those steroids and these new gray-market drugs you write about, like CJC-1295, GHK-Cu, and Ipamorelin? 
The new substances you mention are not steroids at all, but peptides, which are short biological molecules, strings of amino acids like little proteins, that are intended to mimic natural signaling molecules in the human body. The intention of peptide users is to have the muscle-building effects of anabolic steroids without the side effects of infertility, oily skin, acne, liver inflammation, and so on. Insulin is a peptide, so are endorphins, so are the “skinny jabs” such as Mounjaro and Ozempic. The ones used in bodybuilding are not licensed as medications because almost all are experimental—some even have a warning label reading “for experimental use only.” And we don’t have nearly enough data yet to know which ones are going to be safe in the long run, and at which doses.
I’m interested in the mutual influence between medicine and culture—the way we understand our lives through stories—and using experimental peptides before they are understood seems like a classic case of what so many myths warn about: the risks of overreaching. Peptides tinker with the body’s fundamental mechanisms for growth, healing, and inflammation, which can lead to a cancerous overgrowth of tissue and trigger immune problems in some people. Like Icarus flying too close to the sun, or the sorcerer’s apprentice setting into motion a process that then he can no longer control, using these drugs reflects our human urge to push boundaries and try to get something for nothing. But medical history is littered with stories of drugs that were thought to be wonderful when they first appeared and were quickly found to have dark and terrible effects—think thalidomide, Fen-Phen, oxycodone, heroin. 
The world of illicit steroid use seems to have some overlap with the world of maximizing longevity, though as you note steroid use substantially decreases life expectancy. Does the market for longevity drugs pose similar risks for public health? 
There are so many different approaches promoted to enhance longevity, but aging is such a complex process involving so many different elements of our physiology that it’s difficult to say. For example, online you’ll see some sites promoting immune suppressant medications as longevity drugs; one example would be sirolimus. This group of medications is used to prevent the rejection of transplanted organs or to suppress autoimmune conditions, and so the rationale goes that if aging is in part caused by inflammation in the body, then by suppressing the immune system you can live longer. But in the body nothing is ever that simple—the drug also seems to enhance some aspects of the immune system, and going onto it seems to increase the risk of infections, thrombosis, diabetes, lung problems, and so on, all of which might shorten your life, or certainly make it more miserable. There’s a reason it hasn’t been licensed for use as an anti-aging drug.
Another approach by the anti-aging community has been to use different diabetes drugs. I mentioned the GLP-1 agonist drugs above, the “skinny jabs” such as Ozempic—there’s a suggestion that they too might have an effect on lifespan, but it turns out that it’s very tricky to separate the beneficial effect of the drug from the beneficial effect of simply losing weight. We know that obesity is bad for your body’s longevity, so it stands to reason that if you carry less weight on your body you’ll have a good chance of living longer. Another class of diabetes drugs being explored in this regard are the SGL2 inhibitors such as dapagliflozin and empagliflozin—these help your kidneys to push glucose out of the bloodstream and into the urine. Some of my patients feel dreadful on them, but some have no side effects at all. And as with the GLP-1 agonists it’s difficult to tell whether any possible longevity benefits are direct effects of the drug, or whether they’re a side effect of having less sugar in the blood. Meanwhile, they bring their own suite of risks: back pain is a common side effect, as are urine infections, prostatitis, and skin reactions. 
Advertisement
The “ideal” form of the male body has been represented differently across history, from classical sculptures to soldiers to beefcake models in men’s magazines. What ideal do you think young men today have in mind when they do these various looksmaxxing exercises, like “bone-smashing” and “zygo pulling”?
Feeling anxious about how you look is nothing new in human culture! But I think we’re living through an extraordinary time—a biotechnology boom in which it feels to many people, both inside and outside the scientific community, that anything might be possible. And given that culture is now so tied to trends and developments online, fads can take over very quickly and spread around the world in minutes. Modern medicine seems astonishing in its reach and power, and its successes tend to leave the impression that, medically speaking, humanity can do anything and we can reinvent ourselves very easily. From my side of the consultation desk the power of medicine is less impressive. The idea that anything is possible has spilled over into youth culture: if you don’t like your face, change it. There’s a worrying overlap with the impulse to self-harm, which is on the rise among adolescents and taking new forms. We also know that as a behavior it’s contagious: if you know someone who self-harms you are much more likely to do it yourself. The rise of self-harm influencers—people for whom demonstrating their distress online has become a source of income and validation—is a terrifying new development. 
You’re going to explore many of these themes in Gigantic Fantastic, a BBC radio show about bodybuilding that comes out next month. What can listeners expect to learn from the show that wasn’t covered in your essay?
It takes a broader look at myths of strength around the world, at how times (like ours) of economic precarity and conflict foster greater admiration for overt expressions of muscled masculinity. It goes deeper into the history of admiration for strongmen, and how commercial medicine and commercial medical practitioners have always tried to profit from men’s anxiety over their physical and sexual prowess. Some men who use steroids know exactly what they’re doing and take a carefully judged risk, while others are pursuing a fantasy and have lost (or never had) a particular goal for their use. 
The series digs more deeply into the idea of health meaning “wholeness,” and that it must always represent a balance: “health”means something different at eighteen than it does at eighty. Manliness also has different meanings across time periods: in the 1950s “Mr. Britain” and “Mr. America” were still assessed on elements of character as well as of musculature, and I’m curious about how we moved from that culture of self-improvement tied to overall well-being to the kind of steroid-boosted freaky bodies that win those competitions today. Gigantic Fantastic also explores the development of steroids themselves, their physiological effects, as well as other image and performance-enhancing drugs and their dangers. 
As part of my research for the series I attended some of the UK’s harm-reduction clinics to meet and talk with a broad range of men who use steroids. That experience gave me a much deeper understanding of the scene than I’d encountered solely interviewing users in gyms. I also interviewed the clinicians who work in those clinics, to ask their impressions of how the scene has changed over the last twenty years, how they see it evolving into the future, and find out the most effective approaches for addressing the problems of steroid use. The radio series takes in interviews with academic hormone specialists, including the team at Imperial College in London who are conducting some of the most exciting and innovative research in this area. Finally, I speak with natural bodybuilders who are turning away from the synthetic scene: men who insist that you don’t have to be gigantic to look fantastic.

51,2026-10-24,"The takeaway: SK hynix says it still needs to build a US semiconductor fab for Solidigm as rising demand for AI storage strains the subsidiary's production capacity. But it has shelved a potential IPO that could have helped pay for the expansion. The decision shows how the need to expand chip production can clash with the financial conditions required to fund it. 
A new facility would support Solidigm's high-capacity enterprise SSD business. Its existing manufacturing capacity barely covers projected requirements, making additional production a priority for parent company SK hynix.
Dropping the IPO leaves the expansion plans intact but the financing unresolved. SK hynix has stopped reviewing both an IPO and a pre-IPO funding round, citing market volatility and a correction in semiconductor stocks. The company says it will keep evaluating other funding options.
Solidigm plans to expand production of floating-gate 3D QLC NAND memory for its high-capacity data center SSDs. QLC stores four bits per memory cell, while the three-dimensional architecture stacks cells to boost storage density. Solidigm uses both technologies in drives built for enterprise storage.

Growing AI infrastructure deployments are driving demand for high-capacity storage and straining production. To keep up, Solidigm needs to expand its manufacturing capacity, not simply broaden its product lineup.
A new US fab would increase output to support that growth and expand SK hynix's American manufacturing footprint. SK hynix has not said when the fab will open or how much it will produce. The project's scale also remains unclear. Building a modern 3D NAND fab can cost tens of billions of dollars.
SK hynix weighed direct investment, pre-IPO financing and a public offering to fund additional capacity. An IPO would have raised outside equity without adding debt and given Solidigm a market valuation separate from its parent's. SK hynix believes its share price doesn't fully reflect Solidigm's worth, but weak market conditions would leave the subsidiary undervalued.
SK hynix has not said how it will pay for the fab. It could use internally generated cash, borrowing, or a combination of both. Solidigm and SK hynix together hold 21.1% of the booming enterprise SSD market. Their combined share, however, doesn't reveal Solidigm's standalone profitability or how much cash it could contribute to the fab's construction.

52,2026-10-24,"Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality
Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.
TL;DR: The SEC has named tokenization among its fiscal 2027 examination priorities, bringing the systems behind blockchain securities into closer supervisory focus. The staff document sets inspection priorities and creates no new legal obligations.
The SEC’s tokenization agenda now includes a practical question for financial firms: can the infrastructure work reliably when something goes wrong?
In its October 23 announcement, the US regulator placed tokenization among the market developments its examiners will scrutinize during fiscal 2027. The wider program also covers investment advice, complex products, private credit and cybersecurity across more than 34,000 registrants.
For companies building services around tokenized assets, that brings the discussion down from the promise of faster trading to the controls supporting it. A share recorded on a blockchain still needs dependable ownership records, functioning markets and an orderly response to disruption.
The 20-page examination document explicitly includes tokenization among high-risk areas for clearing-agency reviews. In its discussion of Regulation Systems Compliance and Integrity, it lists tokenized securities alongside extended trading hours, advanced AI and quantum computing. Cloud providers and other technology suppliers also feature in those reviews.
That combination matters. Extending trading hours can leave less time for maintenance, while connecting a token to conventional securities records adds another place where systems must agree. The relevant test is whether the complete service remains dependable, including the handoffs between systems.
The document separately emphasizes cybersecurity governance, access controls, incident response and risks from shared technology. It also states that the priorities are staff views, carry no legal force and do not amend existing law. An examination focus should therefore be read as a signal about supervision, rather than a new tokenization license or prohibition.
The context is the SEC’s September innovation exemption for certain tokenized stock venues. That temporary framework requires equivalent shareholder rights, issuer notification and an opportunity to object when an unaffiliated party tokenizes shares. It also imposes trading limits and requirements for public, auditable smart contracts.
Those conditions illustrate why market access and operational supervision move together. A permitted venue can still have weak controls; a technically impressive platform can still mishandle an investor’s rights. The new priorities put attention on that gap.
For investors, the useful distinction is between the token and the service built around it. Questions about who holds the underlying security, how transactions reconcile and what happens during an outage remain relevant alongside the blockchain’s design. Custody and market infrastructure do not become incidental simply because a trade uses a token.
The SEC says its priorities are non-exhaustive and may change during the year. Tokenization’s place in the published agenda nevertheless gives firms a clear reason to expect scrutiny of how their systems operate, as well as what their products promise.
Written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

53,2026-10-24,"The Good Men Project
We are the only international conversation about the changing roles of men in the 21st century.

October 24, 2026 by Mongabay 
 
By Mongabay.com
Fewer than half of tiger poaching and trafficking arrests in Southeast Asia result in convictions or legal penalties, providing little deterrent to an illegal black market that is driving wild tigers towards extinction, reports contributor Sean Mowbray for Mongabay.
Researchers analyzed 259 tiger seizure cases recorded between 2019 and 2024 across seven range countries: Indonesia, Vietnam, Thailand, Malaysia, Cambodia, Myanmar and Laos.
Of those 259 cases, only 110 resulted in prison sentences or fines, according to the report published by Canadian conservation think tank the GuArdean Centre for Conservation Research, wildlife trade watchdog TRAFFIC, and WWF International.
Another 35 cases ended in suspended sentences, while 63 arrests lacked a clear outcome and 48 had no available public data.
The report reveals that law enforcement efforts overwhelmingly target low and mid-level poachers, couriers and local traders, rather than the high-level organizers financing transnational syndicates.
“We’re talking about serious organized crime,” Heather Sohl, tiger trade lead at WWF and report co-author, told Mongabay. “These are criminal networks that are involved in other organized crime, such as human trafficking and money laundering.”
Sohl added that arrests alone are insufficient, “Investigation and case-building is needed to build support for strong prosecutions, and only by looking across the whole criminal justice response can we look to really disrupt these wildlife trafficking networks.”
Vietnam and Indonesia logged the highest conviction numbers, with 51 cases in each country carrying jail time or fines. However, Vietnam also accounted for 33 of the region’s 35 suspended sentences.
Thailand recorded 36 arrests and Malaysia 35, but these yielded just five and nine imprisonments respectively.
Cambodia and Laos documented just one successful prosecution each, while Myanmar reported none.
“It’s really difficult to develop a clear picture of what’s happening in some of the other countries because there’s just no transparency with regards to prosecutions,” report lead author Rachel Boratto, executive director of the GuArdean Centre for Conservation Research, told Mongabay. Boratto said that without follow-through, wildlife laws become “just laws on paper.”
For Vincent Nijman, a wildlife trade researcher and director of U.K.-based EcoVerde Global Consulting, who wasn’t involved in the analysis, the report gives a great overview of illegal wildlife trade, but has some limitations. “The rate of successful prosecution, while high compared to other protected wildlife, remains rather low,” he told Mongabay by email. He acknowledged that limited data is a “major barrier” to understanding the full picture.
Chas Barnes, counter-trafficking lead at wildcat conservation nonprofit Panthera, said that failing to prosecute kingpins undermines overall conservation goals. “Weak law enforcement or weak punishment undermines other important [conservation] interventions,” Barnes stated. “Targeting the low- and medium-level offenders isn’t always the way to go for the maximum disruption”.
Read the full story by Sean Mowbray here. 
Banner image: A Sumatran tiger (Panthera tigris sondaica) in Indonesia. Only a few hundred of the critically endangered species remain in the wild, with numbers dwindling due to poaching and habitat destruction. Image by Rhett A. Butler/Mongabay.

54,2026-10-24,"Menopause symptoms often overlap, making them tricky to manage. You can wake up drenched in sweat, struggle to sleep, or feel unusually anxious without knowing why.
For the first time since 2014, the American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists (ACOG) has updated its guidance1 on treating these symptoms, outlining the medications and non-drug approaches clinicians can consider to help women through this confusing time.
Menopausal hormone therapy (MHT) is one of the most effective treatments for symptoms, like hot flashes and night sweats, that can disrupt sleep.
ACOG recommends generally starting MHT before age 60 or within 10 years of natural menopause to get the most out of it while minimizing side effects. Your clinician can help you weigh the benefits and risks but hormone therapy may not suit people who've had a stroke or certain forms of breast cancer.
If MHT isn't the right fit, or you would rather skip the hormones, ACOG recommends non-hormonal medications:
If you are wary of medication, you may have been inclined to try one of the many natural solutions marketed for menopause symptoms. Soy isoflavones, black cohosh, and other herbal products for menopause have flooded the market in recent years.
However, ACOG states that the available evidence doesn't support the efficacy or safety of phytoestrogens (such as soy isoflavones) or herbal supplements for treating hot flashes or other menopause symptoms. The guidelines also note that these products don't need to be approved by the FDA before hitting the market, so they haven't necessarily been tested for safety, efficacy, or purity.
This doesn't automatically mean every supplement is harmful, but it's a reminder that ""natural"" does not guarantee effectiveness or safety. Tell your clinician about any supplements you're considering, especially if you take other medications.
Beyond pills and patches, ACOG suggests cognitive behavioral therapy (CBT) to improve sleep and mood. CBT teaches people to recognize and change thought patterns that can affect how they cope with symptoms.
Mindfulness practices and regular exercise may also help with menopause-related sleep problems and mood changes. ACOG also lists clinical hypnosis and acupuncture among non-drug options.
Depending on your symptoms and preferences, these approaches may complement, or in some cases replace, medical treatment. Either way, talk with your doctor before making major changes.
If menopause is affecting your quality of life, ACOG wants you to know that you don't have to simply put up with it. Before your next appointment, think about which symptoms are bothering you most and how they're affecting your sleep, mood, or daily routine. Here's a list of a few things to do before your next appointment.
ACOG's new guidelines recognize that menopause care isn't one-size-fits-all. Symptoms vary greatly from woman to woman, and cultural, socioeconomic, and ethnic differences can affect timeliness and quality of care.
That's why it's so important to advocate for yourself when it comes to getting the treatment you need for menopause. You shouldn't have to figure it all out on your own, but arming yourself with knowledge about your options before your next doctor's appointment can help you get the care you deserve. Use ACOG's guidance as a starting point for a conversation with your clinician about what could help you feel better.
*These statements have not been evaluated by the Food and Drug Administration. This product is not intended to diagnose, treat, cure or prevent any disease."
55,2026-10-24,"Gilt funds are back in focus among investors with interest rates rising and bond yields across the world moving up sharply, as reported by ETBureau.
Gilt funds are debt mutual fund schemes that invest in government securities issued by the central and state governments. Long-duration gilt funds generally hold government bonds with maturities of 10 years or more. Since these securities are backed by the government, they carry very low credit risk, with the likelihood of default being minimal. However, their returns can fluctuate significantly with changes in interest rates, particularly because of their longer duration.
Gilt funds generally invest in government securities with longer maturities, making their returns more sensitive to changes in bond yields. When yields rise or fall, the prices of these bonds can move sharply, leading to greater fluctuations in fund returns. In contrast, short-term debt funds invest in bonds and money-market instruments with shorter maturities, making them relatively less sensitive to interest-rate changes and typically resulting in lower price volatility.
While gilt funds carry very low credit risk, they are not entirely risk-free. Their primary risk stems from fluctuations in bond prices, which can affect returns. These prices are influenced by several factors, including RBI interest-rate decisions, inflation, government borrowing, liquidity conditions, US Treasury yields, Federal Reserve policy, crude oil prices, foreign investor flows and geopolitical developments. Changes in any of these factors can trigger movements in bond yields and, in turn, affect gilt fund returns.
Gilt funds tend to perform well when bond yields decline, as bond prices move in the opposite direction to yields. Investors often consider them when interest rates and yields are near their peak, since falling yields can push bond prices up and boost gilt fund returns.
Gilt funds earn returns through interest on government bonds and changes in their prices. When bond yields fall, prices rise, allowing fund managers to book capital gains. Conversely, rising yields push bond prices down, hurting fund returns.
“The current environment offers an opportunity to gradually build exposure to long-duration government bonds,” said Basant Bafna, Head of Fixed Income, Mirae Asset Mutual Fund. According to him, bond yields have already risen sharply, and markets may be pricing in more RBI rate hikes than the central bank ultimately delivers.

Volkswagen, like pretty much all foreign brands, has really been struggling in China in recent years. The whole German auto industry is basically in crisis because their cash cow in China has shifted and now prefers buying cool new EVs from Chinese companies.
Well, Volkswagen decided to team up with young Chinese star XPENG in order to design and develop electric vehicles that would be more compelling and competitive in the Chinese market, and one of those EVs is now coming to market and looks wicked cool and competitive!


The new ID. Unyx 09 has a range of 755 km (469 miles) according to the CLTC rating system, and ranges from 179,900 yuan ($26,939) for the Ultra version to 249,900 yuan ($37,421) for the Max version (and in between them is the Flagship version for 219,900 yuan/$32,929). You can get it as a single-motor sedan or a dual-motor sedan, with the model falling into the midsize class or the large sedan class depending on who you ask.
“The ID. Unyx 09’s front fascia features a closed grille, slim headlights, and the iconic Volkswagen logo. High-tech lighting features include DLP high-definition matrix projection headlights and crystal laser-engraved interactive LED units, which can project lane-guidance light carpets and vehicle-width markers to enhance nighttime safety,” CarNewsChina shares.
“Inside, the cabin’s key features include:
The vehicle is powered by the VLA large-model intelligent driving assistance system, supporting advanced features such as custom parking, remote parking, and trace-back reversing.” That VLA (Vision-Language-Action) system comes courtesy of XPENG.


The car also includes a 91.963 kWh lithium-iron-phosphate (LFP) battery from CATL, 5C ultra-fast charging, and a dual-chamber air suspension system.
What do you think? Can it sell well in China? Can it help spur on a revival for Volkswagen in the largest car market in the world? And, oh, by the way, can we get that car somewhere outside of China at some point?

62,2026-10-24,"DMart founder and ace investor Radhakishan Damani is among India’s richest stock market investors, with his family’s net worth estimated at over $20 billion, according to Forbes. Popularly known as the “retail king of India”, the billionaire publicly holds 14 stocks. Here are his top 10 stock holdings by value, based on data from Trendlyne.
Avenue Supermarts, operator of the DMart hypermarket chain, remains Radhakishan Damani’s largest holding. He held a 67% stake as of September 30, 2026, valued at more than Rs 1.38 lakh crore, according to Trendlyne data. The stock has fallen around 18% in the past month and 15% in 2026 so far. It has declined more than 26% in one year, 14% in three years and over 30% in five years.
NSE shares made their much-awaited market debut on September 24, listing at Rs 1,800 apiece on BSE. This marked a premium of less than 1% over the IPO price of Rs 1,785. Radhakishan Damani was one of the popular stakeholders in the company who sat out on the IPO, making NSE his second biggest stock holding after DMart in terms of holding value. He owns 1.6% stake worth Rs 6,527 crore.

Damani and his family acquired around a 2.4% stake in NBFC Sundaram Finance during the April-June quarter of FY27, which they kept unchanged in Q2 according to Trendlyne data. The stock has dropped around 151% in the past month and more than 25% in 2026 so far. It has gained 25% in three years and 57% in five years.
Damani and his firm Bright Star Investments held a 29% stake in cigarette maker VST Industries as of September 30, 2026, valued at over Rs 974 crore. The stock has fallen around 2% in the past week, 5% in a month and 22% in 2026 so far. It has declined 23% in one year and 33% in three years.
Damani held a 1.5% stake in 3M India through Bright Star Investments, valued at around Rs 480 crore, according to Trendlyne data. The stock has fallen around 17% in the past month and 20% in 2026 so far. The CDMO sub-segment within the pharma sector has proven to be a winner even in the current times of heightened volatility. Earlier this year, we highlighted the positive prospects of Neuland Laboratories, which has transformed into a CDMO-led pharma company over the years from primarily a generic API business. Since then, the stock returned 70 per cent to a peak of ₹24,000 by September 2026; however, post that, it has  corrected 24 per cent now. High valuations at the peak  (53 times one-year forward PE) and the lumpy nature of revenues, consistently highlighted by the company, have contributed to  the correction, along with the general market sell-off. The stock now trades at 42 times one-year forward earnings compared to the last five-year average of 32 times. The premium valuation relative to five-year average can sustain, considering the transition in the  business. The stock can continue to be under pressure in the near term, but this should improve valuation comfort for long-term holdings. Considering the early phase of growth from CDMO opportunities and a gradual foray into peptides, long-term investors can consider the stock  at current and lower levels.
The CMS  (contract manufacturing solutions) division manufactures APIs and intermediates, but for innovator companies that are in the clinical testing phase or have commercialised the products. As on June 30, 2026, the company had 99 projects in the division across commercial (19), pre-registration (7) and the rest in early- to late-stage  trials.
The divisions’ 40 per cent revenue CAGR in FY23-26 essentially drove the consolidated revenue growth of 19 per cent CAGR in the period. The consolidated EBITDA margin expansion from 23 per cent to 28 per cent  supported the 30 per cent EPS CAGR in FY23-26. The CMS division now accounts for 61 per cent of FY26 revenues (₹2,022 crore) from 37 per cent  in FY23. The segment is still in an early phase of growth, as per the management, which implies a further increase in revenue share and margins as the segment grows.
Neuland, prior to the CMS division growth, manufactured APIs and intermediates for high-volume products under Prime division (23 per cent of FY26 revenues) and niche products under Speciality (11 per cent revenue share). The two segments reported slower growth of 7 per cent and -12 per cent CAGR in FY23-26.
CMS operations, which undertakes the CDMO operations, primarily benefit from supplies to a commercialised product as the volume and life of the contract are significantly higher than in-trial products. But the trial pipeline is what develops into a commercial portfolio upon regulatory approvals.
Neuland has 19 commercial products, but the management states that essentially three commercial products, approved in last one-two years, drive bulk of the revenue growth. The three products are expected to grow as the innovator market expands and the strong order book at Neuland should sustain a high growth for next two-three years as per the company.
The company expects two-three more commercial products in the next two years, cumulatively, which should add additional drivers depending on the commercial scale of the product. But the management is confident in the pipeline of products in trials, which should consistently deliver replacements and even drive growth over a five-year period. The company has also expanded its CMS units to accommodate for the higher demand.
The CMS division commercial portfolio expansion will also lead to lower revenue volatility. Currently on a quarterly revenue base of around ₹600 crore per quarter, the start or stop of a commercial order can swing the revenue growth 15-20 per cent either way. With a stable base of several commercialised products, this volatility should also reduce, which is an overhang on Neuland stock. 
Neuland has  now forayed into the segment manufacturing fragments and APIs. Peptides are chains of amino acids, which in turn are building blocks of biological proteins. The synthetic peptides are complex to manufacture and even more complex to purify, which makes peptides a segment with high entry barriers. 
The company has created manufacturing facilities and a portion (Module-I) has been commercialised. The facility will now undergo manufacturing qualifications and other necessary regulatory inspections in the next one year.
Neuland has stated that it has lined up several peptide projects with clients including large pharma companies for the projects. Around 8-10 projects are in study phase, which are products in advanced to early stage for the clients providing a strong possibility of revenue traction for the investment including scope for commercialisation in one-two years.
Neuland also formed a strategic collaboration with Gland Pharma, wherein Neuland — with expertise in complex APIs and Gland in sterile injectables — will be manufacturing complex sterile APIs. Gland will establish the facility as an extension of its site compliant with the US FDA standards for the collaboration.
The company reported 19 per cent/30 per cent CAGR revenue/EPS growth in FY23-26 to ₹2,023 crore/₹364 crore in FY26. It aspires to sustain the revenue growth in the similar range in the next two years. Ramp up in existing CMS commercial products and new additions, peptide division foray and sterile addition should support the growth. With strong tailwinds for the CDMO industry from China +1 supply chain strategies and maturing client relationships, the company has gained on valuation metrics reflected in 42 times one-year forward PE ratio. But with growth in early phase Neuland can support the high valuations. The company has negligible debt and arms it with balance sheet strength for further capital expenditure as well.

64,2026-10-24,"by Chris Allen
Shutterstock
Retail workers expect complaints, but they shouldn’t expect threats over an off-season space heater.
Only one week into the job, this new employee explained why finding one stored heater could take hours.
Instead of accepting the answer, the customer cursed, insulted him, and hinted at fighting him in the parking lot.
Read on to see how his first nightmare customer played out.
So, about a week after starting my retail career I got my first taste of a truly nasty customer.
Things can be difficult when you are new.
It’s an uncharacteristically cold May morning at our big box store. I’m opening our department for the first time with a coworker there for me to shadow.
At this point it’s important to note that we have a rotation of seasonal merchandise which gets wrapped on a pallet and put away for the off-season.
We also have a website which shows store stock (as well as the last known aisle/bin location, which is almost always at least partially incorrect).
I can see how this would cause confusion.
As you might imagine, this leads to a bit of frustration for impatient customers on, say, a cold day in late spring.
So, in walks a couple to my department, a man (henceforth referred to as CH for cold-hearted), and a woman (henceforth not referred to as she was entirely silent during our entire interaction).
He seems more than happy to help.
I notice them looking around the department somewhat frantically.
Me: “Hi! Did you guys need any help today?”
CH: curtly “No.”
Some people are just rude, nothing to worry about.
I then just kind of awkwardly walk away, taken aback and not really sure what to say. But whatever, some people don’t like being pestered while they shop. I went and found something else to work on for a little while.
After a few minutes he flags me down
Oh, now he needs help.
CH: “Where do you keep the heaters?”
Me: “Oh sorry, all of our heaters have either been clearanced out or put away for the season, we don’t have any stock on the shelves”
CH: “Well where’s aisle ‘the aisle we’re in, now the air conditioners‘, bay ‘right in front of us‘? It says you have two of one in particular online.”
Online sites aren’t always accurate.
Me: “Sorry, the site must not have been updated when they took it off the shelf.”
CH: “Well go get me one.”
Is this even something the store wants him to do?
Now. This is not an impossible task, but it is generally really time consuming. This particular winter was not very busy for our store, which meant lots of overstock. So much so that they couldn’t put all of the merchandise in our department’s top stock.
They’d also gone to no effort to categorize where in our huge big box store these miscellaneous pallets of merchandise had been stored.
This could take all day.
So if I wanted to find one exact item, I have to basically walk around the whole store reading pallets until I get lucky and find the model number I’m looking for on the sheet of paper describing every item on the pallet.
At which point I have to find an associate licensed on the tall motor so that we can walk the forklift across the store, close down the aisle, bring down the pallet (with spotter), open it, take out the single box we need, re-wrap the pallet, and then put it back on the shelf.
Hopefully the customer will change their mind.
I had been advised by the senior in our department in this situation to explain to the customer that we could do this, but it could potentially be a couple of hours, so this is what I try to explain to the guy.
CH: “A couple of hours?? Are you serious?”
This is more than generous.
Me: “I’m sorry, but if you want to leave your information we can call you back when we find what you’re looking for.”
CH: “Why don’t you just DO YOUR JOB? I need this heater NOW”
Good thing he has some backup.
At this point my co-worker notices how upset this guy is getting and came over to help. I explained the situation to CW and then let them talk
At one point CH sprinkles in: “And I really don’t APPRECIATE the attitude of your associate over here.”
How did he have a bad attitude?
Me, completely dumbfounded: “Sir I really don’t know what your problem is I’ve just been trying to help this whole time”
CH: “Yeah well just wait until I speak to your manager. You look like a momma’s boy. You’re lucky I’m in here right now and we’re not in the parking lot or cracks neck as if getting ready for a fight.
At that point, they should have asked him to leave.
CW calms down CH and tells him we’ll go find his heater while he waits there. We’re actually able to find it surprisingly fast while we joke about the guy but I’m clearly upset and a little shaken.
The guy was scrawny but had crazy eyes so I was worried he might try to shank me or something. CW ends up grabbing the heater the fast way and not the “technically what we’re supposed to do to not get in trouble” way and goes to take it to the guy. He tells me to cool off in the break room and I do.
Sign up to get our BEST stories of the week straight to your inbox.
People like this are often all talk.
He later explains to our managers what happened to try to vouch for me but apparently the guy never ended up complaining to anyone.
Later my store manager and assistant store manager come over and tell me that next time I should probably just get one of them if a customer’s mad.
You can’t be too careful these days.
I never see the guy again but like the momma’s boy I am was looking over my shoulder walking back to my car after that shift.
If a customer threatens an employee, even like this, they should be asked to leave and never come back.
Let’s see what the people in the comments have to say about this situation.
These people should be called out.

This would have been a good response.

You can’t be too careful.

Yup, I agree.

If you enjoyed this story, check out this post about a lifeguard who saves a child only to be confronted by parents angry about the paperwork.
Right, this is way outside his job description.

This employee stayed calm, explained the difficult inventory process, and even offered to call the customer once the heater was found.
His coworker eventually stepped in, found the item, and told management why the new employee had been so shaken.
Nothing warms up a cold morning like a grown man threatening a retail worker over a space heater.

67,2026-10-24,"Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality
Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.
TL;DR: Crypto exchange BTCC says quarterly futures volume reached $387 billion and its TradFi-linked trading category increased to $16.3 billion, based on company-reported data.
Crypto exchange BTCC says demand for its futures products accelerated during the third quarter, including a sharp increase in contracts linked to traditional financial markets.
The company reported $387 billion in futures trading volume for Q3 2026 in figures released Thursday. It also put trading in its so-called TradFi category at $16.3 billion, up from approximately $2.7 billion in the previous quarter. Both figures come from the exchange's own growth report and should be read as self-reported metrics rather than an independent market-share audit.
Digital-asset trading platforms have increasingly experimented with derivatives that reference equity indices, stocks, commodities and other familiar market exposures. A trader already using an exchange for Bitcoin perpetual contracts may be attracted by the prospect of managing additional positions through the same interface.
The economic difference lies in what is being traded. A derivative tied to a stock or fund does not confer the same rights as owning the underlying security. Counterparty exposure, contract funding and margin requirements can introduce risks that are unrelated to the performance of the named company.
BTCC frames its growth partly around lower trading barriers and new product categories. Lower minimums may broaden access, but they can also make it easier for inexperienced participants to take on leveraged exposure without appreciating liquidation mechanics.
Large headline volumes are common in derivatives because the notional value of contracts traded can far exceed money actually deposited as margin. Heavy turnover therefore does not imply that hundreds of billions of fresh cash entered the exchange during the quarter.
The report also highlights rising registered-account numbers and what BTCC describes as a healthy reserve ratio. Such disclosures are useful starting points, but reserve claims are strongest when backed by independently reviewable liabilities and asset records, not only a promotional percentage.
Comparisons between exchanges are difficult when methodologies for counting derivatives volume, active users and internal market-making activity differ. Traders should pay at least as much attention to spreads, depth, risk controls and withdrawal arrangements as they do to a quarterly volume figure.
The growth report suggests that crypto-native venues see an opportunity in offering broader market exposure. Whether that opportunity becomes lasting revenue will depend on customer retention, regulatory availability and the quality of those products when conditions turn volatile.

69,2026-10-24,"Türkiye remained Europe’s sixth-largest passenger car market in the first nine months of 2026 despite a 15.45 percent decline in sales, while also retaining sixth place in battery-electric car sales.
Sales totaled 627,977 units, placing Türkiye behind Germany, Britain, Italy, France and Spain, according to data compiled from the European Automobile Manufacturers’ Association (ACEA) and the Automotive Distributors and Mobility Association (ODMD).
ACEA’s figures, released on Oct. 22, showed registrations across the EU, European Free Trade Association countries and Britain rising 6.3 percent to 10.56 million. All five markets ahead of Türkiye grew, while Türkiye recorded the steepest percentage decline among the 10 largest markets.
Battery-electric car sales in Türkiye fell 9.1 percent to 120,329 units. Germany, Britain and France led that market, followed by Belgium and Denmark.
Türkiye was the only country among the 10 largest battery-electric markets to record a decline. Sales rose 58.1 percent in Germany, 79.1 percent in France and 30.2 percent in Britain.
Despite the decline in volume, battery-electric cars increased their share of Türkiye’s shrinking market to 19.2 percent from 17.8 percent a year earlier.
Within the EU, battery-electric cars accounted for 22.7 percent of registrations, up from 16.1 percent. Hybrids remained the largest category with a 36.3 percent share, while plug-in hybrids accounted for 10.1 percent.
The combined share of gasoline and diesel cars fell to 28.2 percent from 36.9 percent.
Türkiye’s inclusion in the EU’s proposed “Made in EU” rules remains uncertain following a Franco-German compromise on the plan, which aims to support European production against Chinese competition.
Mercedes-Benz CEO Ola Källenius told the Berlin Global Dialogue this week that modern cars relied on thousands of parts sourced worldwide. Local production requirements could make supply chains more resilient but would also raise costs, he warned.

U.S. intelligence has warned that Iranian plots to kill Americans in the Middle East and elsewhere have reached an advanced stage, according to a memo obtained by Axios.
Türkiye aims to increase its goods and services exports to $450 billion by 2029, Trade Minister Ömer Bolat said on Oct. 24.
Turkish football heavyweights Beşiktaş and Trabzonspor quickly shift their focus to European continental competition on Oct. 22, just three days after going head-to-head in a high-stakes domestic clash."

By Grace Turney 


Updated 15 hours ago,
October 24, 2026


You know the type – the person who always makes you feel exhausted, like they are sucking the life force out of you just by being in their presence. They are called energy vampires, and they feed off of your positivity. Whether they’re constantly complaining, criticizing you, making endless requests, or starting drama, energy vampires can’t get enough of your time and attention – and they never take the hint that they’re draining you. Here are The birth months who are most susceptible to energy vampires, and what these star signs can do to set healthy boundaries.
Those with July birthdays are one of the years most vulnerable to energy vampires because they are so genuinely kind and caring. Guided by the moon, the celestial body of emotions and divine feminine energy, those born in July are loving and maternal. They’re natural caretakers who enjoy being of service to others and making them feel good. 
Energy vampires gravitate toward those born in July because they can sense this zodiac’s empathy. July natives tend to take on other people’s feelings as their own, making themselves responsible for others’ emotions. Energy vampires know that when they’re unhappy, their July-born friend will be unhappy too, and will also do all the emotional work to make them both feel better.
It’s easier said than done, but those born in July can shield themselves from energy vampires by learning to distinguish other people’s emotions from their own. They can be a good friend, partner, or co-worker without catering to people who constantly drain them. July-born individuals must pay attention to how they feel after spending time with someone: do they feel recharged, loved, and inspired? Or do they feel exhausted, judged, and irritated? By recognizing their own emotions, they can identify who is taking advantage of their kindness and sapping their positive energy.
September natives are so susceptible to energy vampires because they love to help, and they just can’t stop themselves from trying to solve other people’s problems. There’s nothing an energy vampire loves more than an endless supply of attention and support from someone who is genuinely invested in resolving their issues.
Ruled by Mercury, the planet of communication and intellect, those born in September are exceptionally bright and perceptive. They are good at fixing things because they quickly and easily spot flaws, and they feel responsible for making the necessary repairs. September-born individuals are also quite patient and good at listening, so energy vampires flock to them to complain about their issues, knowing this birth month will give them the attentive audience they crave. As a logical earth sign, those born in September dispense practical, useful advice – but they don’t stop there. This star sign is willing to get their hands dirty to help their loved ones, doing whatever it takes to give them what they need – what more could an energy vampire ask for?
Those born in September can protect themselves from energy vampires by asking themselves a series of logical questions when they’re feeling overwhelmed: Is this really my problem? Do I have the time, energy, and desire to work on this problem, or am I at capacity? Does it make me feel good to help, or am I feeling drained, resentful, or used? Do I really want to work on this task, or do I just think that no one else can/will do it correctly? By taking stock of their emotions after dealing with someone else’s issues, those born in September can learn to separate what feels good and is a positive use of their time versus an energy vampire draining their life force with their endless problems. 
People born in October are targets for energy vampires because they are natural people-pleasers who hate confrontation. Energy vampires can sniff out someone born in October from a mile away – as one of the most accommodating star signs, these vampires know that those born in October will tolerate a great deal of draining. 
As the sign represented by the scales, those born in October are all about justice and harmony. They want everyone to be treated equally and can’t stand for any sort of unfairness. When an energy vampire approaches with their problems, those born in October want to help make it right. Even once someone born in October realizes they are being used or taken for granted, they struggle to stand up for themselves. They’ll bend over backward to please others, including people they don’t particularly like, just to avoid conflict. 
The best way for those born in October to stop letting energy vampires deplete them is to do the one thing they dislike the most – say no. When energy vampires make yet another outlandish request or start venting about their problems again, those born in October need to hold up a hand and stop them in their tracks. “No” is a complete sentence – they don’t need further explanation than that. Stop answering all their calls, responding to their texts, and giving in to their demands. Yes, the energy vampire may bristle at boundaries – but they will find someone else to leech off of soon enough, and those born in October can invest their energy into the people and activities that fill their cup instead of draining it.

Deals Apple Samsung Motorola

The iPhone Duo officially started shipping this Friday. Apple has warned that there will be limited in-store inventory and online purchases are facing long waiting times. Apple is working on building more units, but that will take some time.
Here’s a look at Apple’s first foldable – it starts at $2,000 for the base 256GB model and goes all the way up to $3,200 for the 2TB model. For comparison, the base iPhone 18 Pro Max (256GB) is $700 cheaper and if portability is a concern, there is the smaller iPhone 18 Pro for $100 less than that.
Now, let’s look at the competition. The Samsung Galaxy Z Fold8 is the only other wide foldable in the US market. With a $200 discount and a lower starting price, it costs $300 less than the iPhone. The Galaxy is thinner (9.7mm vs. 11.3mm) and much lighter (201g vs. 254g) while offering a similar cover display (5.5” vs. 5.4”) and inner display (both 7.6” with similar aspect ratios).
They have nearly identical battery capacities too (4,800mAh vs. 4,883mAh), so endurance will depend on chipset efficiency – it’s a Snapdragon 8 Elite Gen 5 for the Galaxy and an Apple A20 Pro for the Duo. Both phones lack dedicated telephoto cameras, offering only dual 50MP and dual 48MP modules, respectively.
Samsung also has a larger book-style foldable, the Samsung Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra. Despite having a larger 6.5” cover display (a tall and narrow 21:9 panel) and an 8.0” inner display, the Ultra remains thin and light – 8.9mm and 215g (that is 39g less than the Duo). This one packs a 200MP 1/1.3” sensor in the main camera and does have a telephoto lens, even if it’s only a 10MP 3x/69mm module. Here is a more in-depth comparison between the Z Fold8 and the Z Fold8 Ultra.
Last year’s Samsung Galaxy Z Fold7 is $1,350 on Amazon – we saw a similar offer a couple of weeks ago. Now Walmart throws its hat in the ring with a $1,300 price tag. Despite the naming, the sequel to the Z Fold7 is the Z Fold8 Ultra and not the Z Fold8. Why does the new model deserve the “Ultra” moniker? You can read more about that in our versus article. Long story short, there are key improvements to the inner display, battery life, charging and performance.
Google still can’t manage a good discount for its foldable, so we turn to the Motorola Razr Fold as an alternative. This phone is not as thin or light (it measures 10.1mm and weighs 243g), but it has a large 6,000mAh battery with fast 80W wired and 50W wireless charging (vs. 5,000mAh 45W/20W for the Z Fold8 Ultra). It uses the non-Elite Snapdragon 8 Gen 5 chipset and promises 7 OS updates, the same as the Galaxy. The phone has a 50MP main with a 1/1.28” sensor, a higher resolution 50MP 3x/71mm telephoto and a 50MP ultra-wide camera.
While we’re in the mood for foldables, the Samsung Galaxy Z Flip8 is still at $1,050 – we’ve seen it at under $1,000 and we think that is the better price for it.
In the meantime, last year’s Motorola Razr Ultra 2025 is back down to $700. The phone has its flaws – we nod towards the old Snapdragon 8 Elite as we say that – but it’s cheaper than the Z Flip8 while offering more RAM and storage. It has a bigger battery with faster charging too.
Samsung.com is selling the new Galaxy Tab S12+ and Tab S12 Ultra with a $100 discount available via code PYPL100. These tablets have 12.6” and 14.6” screens (120Hz OLED), respectively, and 10,600mAh and 11,600mAh batteries. Both run on last year’s Dimensity 9500 and have full DeX support.
In the comments under a recent poll, someone made an interesting observation – why buy a Galaxy Tab S12 Ultra when you can have a MacBook for the same amount of money? Well, the new MacBook Air 13” is actually cheaper than the Ultra tablet, unless you do a trade-in for the Galaxy slate. It’s powered by the Apple M5 chip (it was announced in March, before the M6 came out). The base 16/512GB model launched at $1,100, but that was before a recent price hike added $200 to that number.
For desk work, the latest Apple Mac mini comes with the M6 chipset and saves a few hundred bucks by not having a screen, battery or keyboard. Plus, you get more I/O – three Thunderbolt 4 ports (vs. two), a full-size HDMI and a 2.5Gb Ethernet port.
By the way, Apple recently refreshed its Apple TV 4K streaming device. The 4th gen model costs double what the 3rd gen did at launch (though that too was hit by the price hike). It has last year’s Apple A19 chipset (the GPU is 2x faster than the A15 in the old model), plus 128GB storage and gigabit Ethernet as standard.
We may get a commission from qualifying sales.
Our folding wallets remained closed.

75,2026-10-24,"When you invest or speculate in financial markets, both may involve risk; but the difference lies in the approach you take. It is also about having a clear and fundamental understanding of what you are doing with your hard-earned money. Sensible investors focus on their long-term financial goals, business fundamentals, and current economic reality.
On the other hand, speculators act differently, since they focus more on chasing quick returns based on price movements, market rumours or short-term trends. That is why, to ensure meaningful success, it is vital for day-to-day financial market participants to avoid common mistakes, as this approach can also help investors make more disciplined and meaningful financial decisions.
Hence, the focus should be on saving money before thinking about generating quick money. Keeping these basics in mind, here are common mistakes investors should avoid to succeed in their wealth-creation journey.
Mistake
Do
Don't
Note: These mistakes are illustrative in nature. Investors should seek professional guidance from a certified financial advisor before investing.
Therefore, successful investing does not mean avoiding every loss or predicting every market movement correctly. No one can achieve perfection in investing. Investing, hence, means making informed decisions, managing risks properly, and remaining consistent with a well-defined financial plan.
Even fundamentally strong investments can underperform due to market conditions, and diversification cannot completely eliminate the risk associated with market investments.
That is why investors should also review their portfolios and asset allocation (investments in stocks, bonds, fixed deposits, gold, and other asset classes) periodically and ensure they remain aligned with their long-term financial objectives.
The portfolio should be developed based on the guidance of a certified financial advisor, on changing circumstances and risk-taking appetite. Short-term market fluctuations should not automatically trigger changes to a long-term strategy.
In summary, the difference between intelligent investing and speculative investing is not simply how frequently someone trades; it is also not about how much money an individual can make in a short period of time, but about whether decisions are based on meaningful research, valuation, and clear investment objectives.
Patience, discipline, understanding of investment fundamentals and limitations, and having realistic return expectations can help investors build wealth more substantially over time.
Disclaimer: This article is for general information only and does not constitute investment advice. Investments in securities are subject to market risks. Investors should assess their financial goals and risk tolerance and consult a qualified financial adviser before making investment decisions.

The car was finished from the factory in Maroon, and it is believed to have been repainted in its current shade of dark red and silver at some point under prior ownership. Exterior details include 442-specific graphics, gold-finished accents, a hood ornament, and splash guards. The power antenna was replaced in September 2022.

The chrome-finished 15″ Super Stock III wheels have gold accents and are mounted with BFGoodrich tires that were installed in March 2022. The car is equipped with power steering, and stopping power is provided by front disc and rear drum brakes.

The front bucket seats and rear bench are trimmed in Maroon cloth with vinyl sides and white accents. Woodgrain trim adorns the cabin, the door panels have 442 logos, and additional interior details include a power-adjustable driver’s seat, a Delco AM/FM/cassette stereo, cruise control, air conditioning, and power windows and locks. The left-side dash speaker is loose.

The two-spoke leather-wrapped steering wheel is mounted on a tilting column and frames an 85-mph speedometer, a tachometer, and auxiliary gauges. The five-digit odometer shows 32k miles, although the Carfax report shows a potential odometer rollback in June 2004 along with multiple six-digit mileage entries. Approximately 406 miles have been added under current ownership.

The 307ci V8 was equipped from the factory with a four-barrel carburetor and was factory rated at 170 horsepower and 250 lb-ft of torque. The throttle position sensor was replaced in September 2022, the water pump and oil pan gasket were replaced in December 2022, and the radiator was repaired in April 2026, at which time an oil change was performed.

Power is sent to the rear wheels through a four-speed automatic transmission.

The reproduction window sticker lists factory colors, equipment, and a total vehicle price of $13,602.41. The owner’s manual and service records are included in the sale.
The Carfax report shows no accidents or other damage. It also lists the aforementioned potential odometer rollback.

Abhijeet Mishra / SamMobile
Galaxy Z Fold 8 vs Fold 8 Ultra
For the seventh year now, Samsung has been running its largest annual promotion for customers across the globe. 

Samsung Week 2026 runs through to November 1 and provides customers in 63 countries across the globe with special purchase benefits when they buy products through the company's online store.

Throughout Samsung Week 2026, customers can get more mileage out of their Samsung Rewards Points on Samsung.com. These points can be utilized for purchases of devices, accessories, digital content, and services available through the Galaxy Store, Samsung Art Store, and Samsung Food.

In addition to the above, customers who purchase a new Galaxy Z series, Galaxy S26 FE or Galaxy Tab S12 series devices will receive a six-month subscription to Google AI Pro for free.

Samsung.com-exclusive colors of Green Shadow for the Galaxy Z Fold 8 Ultra, Pistachio for the Galaxy Z Fold 8, and Mint for the Galaxy Z Flip 8 are available as well throughout this promotion. 

The company is also promoting Samsung Week 2026 through large digital billboards at major locations in key cities, including Times Square in New York City and Piccadilly Circus in London.

Offers and deals available during this promotion will vary by market, so make sure you check out Samsung's online store for your region to get access to the latest offers.

82,2026-10-24,"
by Gary Leff on October 24, 2026 
A group of JetBlue’s creditors has hired bankruptcy advisers after $2 billion in bonds backed by its TrueBlue frequent flyer program fell to 42 cents on the dollar. The airline’s shareholders have already lost most of their investment. Now lenders are organizing to protect theirs.
The Wall Street Journal reports that the creditors are working with PJT Partners.  JetBlue shares closed Friday at $2.63. I discussed this week whether a stock falling dropping below $2 is a bankruptcy warning. Having lenders hire restructuring specialists is.. a more concrete signal. 

The bonds had been trading near 80 cents only earlier this month.  Even at 84 cents that was a concern and and they’d begun heading south earlier this month.
These are secured creditors with a claim against JetBlue’s best, highest margin business – its frequent flyer program, and the revenue from its Barclays credit card.
It suggests risk in $197.5 million annual interest that should be its most likely to pay out.
JetBlue, for its part, says it’s confident in its strategy and progress toward profitability. 



Should JetBlue Have Considered Leaving The Airline Business?
In July 2022, I wrote that JetBlue should consider exiting the airline business instead of buying Spirit.  
The argument being made for the acquisition was that JetBlue had to grow, couldn’t grow enough organically, and therefore had no choice but to buy another airline. That never made sense to me. A company doesn’t owe management a larger airline to run. It owes shareholders a sensible use of their money.
Buying Spirit meant paying a large premium, raising its costs, removing seats from its planes, spending money on new interiors, and undertaking a complicated integration – all while increasing the costs on the Spirit side to build a JetBlue operation that wasn’t making money.  It seemed like if there wasn’t a better way to earn a return by flying then returning the money to shareholders or investing it elsewhere would be better. 
After they lost the American Airlines partnership and the Spirit deal, I argued they needed to fix their operation, rebuild Boston, retreat from weak flying and find a new larger airline partnership. They actually did some of that, while costs increased.
 



I recently included Robin Hayes among the worst airline leaders in history. He inherited a product customers liked and valuable positions in New York and Boston. He left poor reliability, a degraded passenger value proposition and wasted time and money on a failed acquisition that paid Spirit shareholders more than $500 million without buying the airline.  (Actually succeeding in buying the airline – after the logic for it to support growth allowed by partnerin with American was lost – would have been even worse.)
In 2023, Hayes acknowledged they’d neglected the potentially most profitable avenue for their business, TrueBlue, and they were playing catch-up – an extraordinary error for an airline whose strongest markets are in major financial and spend centers!  Now Joanna Geraghty is trying to fix the business while financing the time necessary to make that happen. 
JetBlue Is Burning Cash Even Before Hitting Their Wall of Debt
JetBlue’s June 30 balance sheet showed $2.2 billion in unrestricted cash and investments, an undrawn $600 million credit line, and $8.5 billion in debt and finance leases.  Scheduled principal repayments were due:



Period
 
Amount




Remainder of 2026
 
$236 million


2027
 
$478 million


2028
 
$582 million


2029
 
$1.835 billion


2030
 
$656 million


Thereafter
 
$4.691 billion



That leaves out interest and the cash they need to run and improve the airline.  About 67% of their assets at book value are mortgaged, and it’s not clear how much they can raise against the rest.  They claim $6 billion in unencumbered assets but they surely can’t raise another $6 billion, and their financing costs are likely to only go up. 
And there are severe restrictions in their mortgage on the frequent flyer program including at least $750 million of quarter-end liquidity and a maximum senior debt to collateral ratio of 62.5%. Failing either test would divert credit card revenue to accelerate repayment of debt.    



Credit card processors could also hold back revenue from ticket sales as a hedge against continued operations, which only serves to accelerate any cash crunch.  We’ll learn more with JetBlue’s third quarter earnings call on Tuesday. 
JetBlue’s Turnaround Is A Race Against Time
JetBlue is concentrating on their East Coast leisure network, says they’re improving reliability, adding premium products to boost revenue (while further degrading standard coach) and trying to earn more their credit cards with lounges and updates to the frequent flyer program. 
Second quarter earnings showed higher revenue, though slightly lagging the industry, but real improved cash from Barclays with new premium card acquisitions up almost 40% (as you’d expect – it didn’t make sense to apply for the high annual fee credit card whose primary benefit was lounge access before there were any lounges to access). Fort Lauderdale revenue per available seat mile increased 11% despite nearly 40% more capacity.
They still had a $141 million operating loss in the second quarter, so they wouldn’t have made money even if interest expense was zero.  The cost of fuel is up, but JetBlue’s problems long predate that.
 



United May Want JetBlue In Bankruptcy
On United’s Wednesday earnings call, Jamie Baker asked Scott Kirby why United shouldn’t be a potential solution for JetBlue as it considers restructuring. Kirby replied:
So I’m not going to create any new news. I just have nothing new to add on that.
Kirby, who opines on almost anything – including wanting a national wealth tax and near-100% inheritance tax – simply moved on, refusing comment.  

I wrote in May that a United acquisition would make sense only in the context of a controlled bankruptcy.  Kirby had said that JetBlue’s losses were too large and that they’d need an improvement of about 25 percentage points in its margins to make an acquisition work.
JetBlue’s market cap is now less than $1 billion.  There’d be an acquisition premium, but buying the shares cheap doesn’t remove the debt which approaches $9 billion.
Combining the airlines also means integration costs and bringing employees onto United’s higher pay scales. 
United would value New York JFK, JetBlue’s position in Boston, and a South Florida hub.  It already gets some benefit from its JetBlue partnership.  And they’re well positioned to buy assets to provide liquidity.  
Most of JetBlue’s assets are pledged, but shedding some of those that United is less interested in or cramming down obligations against them, could make them a more attractive target.  United doesn’t necessarily need the planes!
Topics on this pageBankruptcyCorporate debtJetBlueTrueBlueAmerican AirlinesBarclaysBostonFort LauderdaleJoanna GeraghtyJohn F. Kennedy International AirportNew YorkScott KirbyThe Wall Street JournalUnited Airlines+10 more

Buying Spirit meant paying a large premium, raising its costs, removing seats from its planes, spending money on new interiors, and undertaking a complicated integration – all while increasing the costs on the Spirit side to build a JetBlue operation that wasn’t making money.  It seemed like if there wasn’t a better way to earn a return by flying then returning the money to shareholders or investing it elsewhere would be better. 
After they lost the American Airlines partnership and the Spirit deal, I argued they needed to fix their operation, rebuild Boston, retreat from weak flying and find a new larger airline partnership. They actually did some of that, while costs increased.
 



I recently included Robin Hayes among the worst airline leaders in history. He inherited a product customers liked and valuable positions in New York and Boston. He left poor reliability, a degraded passenger value proposition and wasted time and money on a failed acquisition that paid Spirit shareholders more than $500 million without buying the airline.  (Actually succeeding in buying the airline – after the logic for it to support growth allowed by partnerin with American was lost – would have been even worse.)
In 2023, Hayes acknowledged they’d neglected the potentially most profitable avenue for their business, TrueBlue, and they were playing catch-up – an extraordinary error for an airline whose strongest markets are in major financial and spend centers!  Now Joanna Geraghty is trying to fix the business while financing the time necessary to make that happen. 
JetBlue Is Burning Cash Even Before Hitting Their Wall of Debt
JetBlue’s June 30 balance sheet showed $2.2 billion in unrestricted cash and investments, an undrawn $600 million credit line, and $8.5 billion in debt and finance leases.  Scheduled principal repayments were due:

$1.835 billion
